EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Recommendation: Staff recommends approval of the Special Use Permit, subject to the
conditions of this Staff Report.  The Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission
recommends approval of the Staff Report, subject to the conditions of this Staff Report.
Location: The property is located in the Pinetop-Lakeside area, on APN# 212-15-002H.
Size: One (1) parcel totaling 5.15 acres.
Supervisor District: IV.
Zoning: A-General and C-R.
Property Owners: Somerset Investment Properties, LLC.
Applicant: Brian Lemmermann.
Representative: Zach Barlow.
REQUEST DESCRIPTION:
Consideration of a request by Ironside Engineering & Development, Inc. for a Special Use
Permit to expand an existing storage unit facility to an adjacent, 5.15-acre parcel in the
Lakeside area. APN#: 212-15-002H, T9N, R22E, S15 of the GSRM. Owner: Somerset
Investment Properties, LLC. Agent: Zach Barlow.

<!-- page 395 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e2 | 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD:
The subject property is located in the Pinetop Lakeside area.  Adjoining this property to the
northwest and northeast is vacant land.  To the south of this property is existing EZE Storage
and Penrod RV and Mobile Homestead.  Also to the south of the property is J&S Patriot
Automotive.
BACKGROUND/HISTORY:
The applicant is requesting to expand an existing storage unit facility to a 5.15-acre parcel. The
proposal would be an appropriate usage within the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance, if
approved for a Special Use Permit.
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:
The proposed project will develop 5.15 acres of land on APN # 212-15-002H to expand the
existing EZE Storage facility to the south of the property.  The expansion is primarily offset
from State Route 260 and will be significantly shielded from the roadway by existing
commercial development and the existing EZE Storage facility.  There will be approximately
ten (10) storage unit buildings of varying sizes.  The project boundary is set to have a six (6)
foot site obscuring fence while the remainder of the borders will be enclosed due to some of
the storage unit buildings being used as the “fortress” style development.
ZONING DISTRICT:
ARTICLE 3 – (A-GENERAL) – GENERAL ZONING DISTRICT
Section 301 – Purpose
The main purpose of this A-General Zone is to provide for all the unincorporated area of the County,
except land within the boundary of a reservation and outside the authority of Navajo County not
otherwise designated for some other specific zone, to be included in the "A-General Zone", by this
Ordinance. No subdividing shall be conducted or approved in the "A-General Zone", without prior
re-zoning of the land so intended. Uses permitted in the "A-General Zone", include farm and non-
farm residential uses, farms, recreational, institutional, commercial and industrial uses as
specifically listed in this Article. Other uses may be permitted as Special Uses under Article 20.
ARTICLE 15 – (C-R) – COMMERCIAL-RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT
Section 1501 - Purpose.
The principal purpose of this zoning district is to provide for shops and services in convenient
locations to meet the daily needs of families in the immediate residential neighborhoods and
wholesaling or distribution activities in locations where there is adequate access to major streets or
highways. Principal uses permitted in this zoning district include retail and wholesale commerce,
commercial entertainment, and some uses of a light manufacturing nature.

<!-- page 396 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e3 | 
ZONING ORDINANCE:
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments
Section 2001 – Special Uses
The Board of Supervisors may permit as a Special Use the following uses in zoning
districts from which they are otherwise prohibited by this Ordinance, unless any such
use is further prohibited by this Article.
32.  Storage of manufactured homes, travel trailers, boats, and aircraft.
35.  Warehousing and storage, mini-storage, including outdoor storage, i.e.,
recreational and equipment storage, public utilities, but not including the
warehousing and storage of explosives or junk.
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:
Comprehensive Plan
Land Use Goals and Policies
Land Use Goal #2
Encourage infill opportunities to capitalize on existing infrastructure and reduce growth-
related costs
 The applicant wishes to extend the current EZE Storage.
Land Use Policy #1
Navajo County supports continued coordination with federal, state, and local agencies to
ensure a balanced mix of land uses in the region.
 Applicant indicated they will coordinate with ADOT to secure an encroachment
permit for the proposed emergency access driveway entrance off of State Route
260.
Land Use Policy #16
Navajo County supports development that provides buffers between different land uses, such as
increased setbacks, walls, fences, and landscaping.
 The project has a proposed six (6) foot security fence whenever there is not a storage
unit building set up as the barrier along the perimeter of the site.
Land Use Policy #18
Navajo County supports the provision of multiple access routes or alternative designs for all
developments, regardless of size or area.
 The applicant has indicated that the project will improve safety by providing another

<!-- page 397 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e4 | 
highway access point to the EZE Storage Facility.
Analysis:
The proposed special use meets all relevant goals and policies of the Navajo County Zoning
Ordinance as described above. This project, and its submittal, meet all requirements of Article
20 of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance, which regulates Special Uses-Planned Unit
Development.
Planning and Development Services Staff has received zero (0) public comment for the project
as of June 23rd, 2025.
At the June 19th, 2025, Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission Hearing, the Navajo
County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously recommended approval of this Special
Use Permit subject to the conditions recommended by Staff which are contained in this Staff
Report.
FINDINGS OF FACT:
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona
Revised Statutes and Article 29.
This item has been completed.
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan.
The proposed project meets all applicable goals within Navajo County’s Comprehensive
Plan, as noted within this report.
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning
district in which the property is located.
The proposed use is compatible with permissible uses within the A-General and C-R zoning
districts.
4. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of
properties in the vicinity of the proposed use.
The proposed use has been determined to be compatible with current and likely future uses
per the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance.
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health,
safety, and general welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use.
Given the nature of the property and the zoning in the area, there should be minimal
adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general welfare of properties in the vicinity
of the proposed use.

PLANNING AND DEVELOPMENT SERVICES RECOMMENDATION:
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff recommend approval of the Special
Use Permit, subject to the following conditions. The Navajo County Planning and Zoning
Commission recommends approval of the Special Use Permit, subject to the following
conditions:
1. The applicant shall obtain any required Navajo County permits, including, but not limited to,
building, sewer connection, grading, drainage, and sign permits.
2. No building or structure shall not be occupied prior to complete compliance with all
appropriate Building Department requirements.
3. All lighting on the property shall be shielded to reflect downward, be motion-activated, and
be in conformance with the Navajo County Lighting Ordinance.
4. A Site Lighting Plan prepared by an Electrical Engineer licensed in the State of Arizona
(required by code for commercial projects) shall be submitted.
5. Fencing shall be approved by Navajo County Planning and Development Services, prior to
installation.
6. Hours of operation will be 9:00 am to 5:00 pm Monday through Saturday.
7. A Drainage Study, which meets the Navajo County Drainage Policy, shall be submitted and
approved.
8. A Grading and Drainage Plan shall be submitted and approved by Navajo County Public
Works prior to the issuance of a building permit for any of the proposed structures.
9. A Geotechnical Report for a gravel roadway section shall be submitted and approved.
10. A copy of the ADOT Encroachment Permit for the driveway connection to State Route 260
shall be submitted to Engineering.

<!-- page 399 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e6 | 
11. The Fire Department shall approve the fire suppression design for the development.
12. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site plan.  Any
expansion or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to this Permit.
13. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period, from the date
of Board approval, this permit shall become null and void.
14. This Special Use Permit shall be personal to the permittee and applicable only to the specific
use and to the specific property for which it is issued.  Upon completion and final inspection
by the Planning and Development Services Director (or designee) of any authorized
structures, signifying that all zoning and site development requirements imposed in
connection with the permit have been satisfied, the Special Use Permit shall thereafter be
transferable and shall run with the land, whereupon the maintenance of special conditions
imposed by the permit, as well as the compliance with other provisions of this title, shall be
the responsibility of the property owner.
15. This Special Use Permit shall run with the land pending compliance with all County, State,
and Federal requirements.
16. This Special Use Permit and the subject site shall be reviewed and inspected by Planning and
Development Staff on the fifth (5 th) anniversary of the Board of Supervisors’ approval and
every five (5) years thereafter to ensure the site is being utilized as approved and is in
compliance with all conditions contained herein.  If the subject site is found to be out of
compliance with these conditions, this Special Use Permit and its privileges may be revoked.

WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit for EZE Storage Expansion was
duly filed on April 16, 2025, by Zach Barlow to expand an existing storage unit facility to a 5.15
acre parcel in the Lakeside area; and
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County:
APN # 212-15-002H, Township 9 North, Range 22 East, Section 15 of the Gila and Salt River
Base and Meridian; and

Planning Division of the Planning & Development Services Department in connection with Planning
related reviews and permits.
Background:
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors adopted a comprehensive update to the Navajo County Zoning
Ordinance on Tuesday, June 24th, 2025.  As a result of this update, some minor amendments to the
Planning and Development Services Fee schedule are required in order to keep updated processes in
alignment with the adopted fee schedule.  These processes and the suggested fees are below: 
Zone Change with Comprehensive Plan Amendment: $2,000.00 plus $50.00 per acre with a fee cap
of $25,000.00.
Special Use Permit for Renewable Energy Generation Facilities: $600.00 plus $50.00 per acre with
a fee cap of $25,000.00.
Seasonal RV Permit: $400.00.
Recommendation: 
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff recommends approval of the amendment to the
existing Planning and Development Services Fee Schedule.

Title: Attachment E to Navajo Nation IGA for N9806 Road Maintenance Materials (Dist II)
Submitted For: Madhav Mundle, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Nicole Reske, Tribal Government Relations Liaison
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Nicole Reske
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Attachment E to the Navajo Nation Intergovernmental Agreement for N9806 roadway
maintenance work.
Background:
N9806 is located in the Indian Wells Chapter. This route needs gravel placement and related maintenance
work to improve the surface conditions. The route is primarily used by school buses, work commuters,
first responders and residents. In addition, this route is part of the Navajo Division of Transportation
(NDOT) regional road maintenance program. The County will reimburse NDOT for the material cost of
Type C base gravel material (approximately 2 inches thick) and road stabilizer on two miles of Navajo
Route 9806. The project limits are shown in the attached map.  The NDOT personnel will be responsible
for scheduling and performing all maintenance activities related to the scope of work.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 2026
Budgeted Y/N:
Amount Requested: 170,000
Fiscal Impact:

N9806  is  located  in  the  Indian  Wells  Chapter.  During  poor  weather  the  road 
becomes muddy and dangerous.   An estimated two miles of gravel placement is 
being proposed to establish a more stabilized, smoother road. 
The route is primarily used for school buses, work commuters, first responders and 
residents.  In  addition,  this  road  is  part  of  the  Navajo  DOT  regional  road 
maintenance program. 
Scope of Work:  
Navajo DOT will grade the roadway, re‐establishing original lines and grades, add 
gravel  material  and  have  a  road  stabilizer  product  applied  to  the  road.   Navajo 
County  will  reimburse  for  Type  C  base  gravel  material  (approximately  2  inches 
thick 10,560’ x 22’ wide x 2” deep = 4,356 tons) and road stabilizer for placement 
of on two miles of Navajo Route 9806. The start of the project will be from the 
intersection  west  ward.  The  gravel  material  placement  will  stabilize  the  road’s 
profile in dry and wet conditions for futures use. The labor and maintenance will 
be performed by Navajo DOT maintenance personnel.

To the Intergovernmental  Agreement Between
THE NAVAJO NATION
AND
NAVAJO COUNTY
The ATTACHMENT E is approved pursuant to the Intergovernmental Agreement (IGA) between
the Navajo  Nation and  Navajo  County, effective date of April 3rd, 2024. The IGA was assigned
Contract No . MOU279 .  This ATTACHMENT  E is hereby  approved  by the signatures
below in  accordance  with Paragraph 1 .1 Initial and  Additional PROJECTS of the IGA between the
Navajo  Nation  and Navajo  County  (Contract  No.   MOU279  ) and provides for the following :
SCOPE OF WORK:
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors, by motion duly passed at the Board's regular meeting on
July 15th, 2025, has approved the expenditure of up to $170,000.00  in District 2 Special Project
Road Funds to be used for the purchase of Type C gravel material & stabilizer for N9806 Gravel
Project. Such material shall be used for road maintenance activities wh ich are located within the
Indian Wells Chapter on the Navajo Reservation, in Navajo County, within Supervisorial District 2.
For any material acquired in whole or  in part with the Allocated Funds, Navajo County
(County) shall have no responsibility  or liability whatsoever in regard to such material. The
Nation shall be responsible for identifying , prioritizing , scheduling, and performing all
maintenance  activities for which the Allocated Funds are used in whole or in part. It is
understood that the County's sole obligation hereunder is to make payment of the Allocated
Funds.
This Attachment shall be valid effective  July 15 th, 2025,  through  June 30,  2026. Any Allocated
Funds  that are  unused  as of June 30, 2026, shall no longer  be available for distribution
pursuant to this ATTACHMENT E.
The material  purchased by  the County with  the allocated funds shall be delivered to the project
site in Indian Wells  at a time and location to be determined :

INTRODUCTION	
The Navajo County Capital Improvement Program (CIP) is a five-year 
plan for physical improvements to th e County’s infrastructure. The CIP 
sets forth proposed expenditures for systematically constructing, 
upgrading, modernizing, and replacing of public infrastructure. This 
document contains projects which are funded based on revenue 
projections. There are many other id entified needs that have not been 
included because sufficient funds could not be identified at the time of 
program development. The project prioritization is based on the need for 
the project and the available resource s needed to complete the project. 
The CIP is reviewed and updated each year by the Public Works staff and 
presented to the Board of Supervisors for their review and approval. The 
completed projects are removed, new projects are proposed, and 
scheduled projects are accelerated or  moved to later years based on the 
availability of resources. 
The CIP consists of the Public Works Department’s capital projects 
related to the divisions listed below: 
1. ENGINEERING 
2. FLOOD CONTROL 
3. HIGHWAY MAINTENANCE 
4. FLEET MANAGEMENT

Chip Seal $412,500 $422,500 $432,500 $442,500 $452,500 $2,162,500

III, IV & V
Slurry Seal is a site‐mixed emulsion of oil and sand to provide a wearing surface, prevent water 
infiltration, and prevent asphalt oxidation. Chip seal is a pavement surface treatment that combines one 
or more layer of asphalt with one or more layer of fine aggregate.  Fog Seal is an application of an asphalt 
emulsion onto an existing pavement surface to renew oxidized pavement surfaces. Crack Seal is a 
surface treatment to exisitng asphaltic pavement to fill individual pavement cracks to prevent entry of 
water or other substances such as sand, dirt, rocks or weeds.
The exisiting trucks have reached end of their useful life and the new vehicles will help mainatin the 
current level of roadway maintenace operations.

NAVAJO COUNTY
Public Works

<!-- page 457 -->
Purpose
Capital Improvement Program
Provides a roadmap for constructing, upgrading, and replacing essential public
infrastructure.
Serves as a strategic tool for meeting both near-term and long-term infrastructure goals.
Establishes a prioritized plan for addressing major capital needs over a defined planning
period.
Supports informed decision-making and the efficient allocation of County resources.
Aligns infrastructure investments with community growth, safety, and service needs.

Title: Winslow Levee Update

Subject:
Project update for the Winslow Levee at the Little Colorado River.
Background: 
The Winslow Levee reconstruction project is a joint project between the US Army Corps of Engineers
and the Navajo County Flood Control District. This presentation is to provide project status and the design
development draft schedule for the Winslow Levee reconstruction project.
